| D244 | **One height ceiling in the editor, on every map** (Kyler, 2026-09-26; amends D172 for editing; recorded in D158). (1) Every tool that raises land (the brushes, the four forces, and Claude's steps, M12 staying ready per D134) can go up to D172's tall maximum on any map, not just 16 on standard maps: one ceiling everywhere, and nothing about it shows in the interface. (2) A map whose land goes above 16 simply becomes a tall map: it gets D172(4)'s note in its description and is exported and validated as tall, everywhere a map's standard/tall kind shows; if editing or undo brings it back to 16 or below, it is a standard map again. (3) Generation is unchanged: the generator's Verticality plan (D172(3)) and Real places' standard/tall option (D172(2)) still decide how tall a generated map starts. (4) Round 2's Erupt fit stays exactly as built; under the higher ceiling it only comes into play on high ground (this replaces round 2's default "the forces' ceiling stays 16"). **Step 1, before building:** an in-game DGM Probe check (D218; M9a keeps its priority on the machine) of maps made in the editor as a player would (a volcano erupted on low ground with its summit at the ceiling and a river past it; a waterfall from high ground; land near the top on a 256² map): they load, the terrain is right, the water settles as the editor showed, and what the probe can check of building on the upper slopes; and what Timberborn's own map editor does to land above 16 when a tall map is opened and saved. Anything that breaks play stops the work until Kyler decides; anything the probe can't check goes on his hands-on checklist. **Step 2:** built on `feature/forces` alongside Unleash (D239): the one ceiling in the editor, the worker, the forces core, the brushes and Claude's step limits; standard/tall by the land's height (the note, the export, both validators; the in-game editor finding in the tall note, in plain words); frame times at 256² with land near the ceiling; tests (a standard map erupted past 16 exports and validates as tall, undo returns it to standard, every brush and force reaches the ceiling and never passes it, Erupt against the prototype at the new ceiling, the Claude suite); captures. **Step 3:** on the preview with Unleash, on Kyler's single checklist (Erupt on low and high ground, Quake's Lift on a high map, anything from step 1 that needs his hands); not released until he has tried it. | Kyler: the demo he approved had about 19 levels of room; at 16 a typical editor map (standing at 6–12) gives Erupt 4–10, so most eruptions come out scaled down, and Lift, Raise and Craterize's peaks hit the same wall. D172: height matters for fun maps. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-26 |
| D245 | **Real places are kept on their own land** (Kyler, 2026-09-26; PR #35; amends D214's "a place that still can't work is dropped"). Kyler: "The beauty of real places is the unique composition of terrain features. It's wonderful if it's playable, but not meeting the cutoff should not disqualify the place; the editor can fix shortcomings if a place is interesting enough to want to play." (also in docs/PERFECT.md). (1) A place is kept on its own land: failing a playability check never drops a place, swaps its land for another part of its region, or changes its height mapping or scale. (2) Only the absolutes gate a place: the file loads and plays exactly as the editor shows it (every correctness check, never weakened), and the starting-logs floor, met by planting groves as now, never by dropping or moving land. (3) Every other check becomes information. The start goes to the best spot its own land offers (moving it toward water as D214 does is fine); anything still short ships as it is. (4) **A place's note** (on its card, by the checks dot, and under its title on the review sheet) covers only what would sink a player who goes straight to the game: no water a pump can reach from the start; too little wood near the start (Minimum starting wood within 20 tiles); water that keeps moving. The everyday advisories (drought, reservoir, clean water in a badtide and the like) get no note: nearly every Timberborn map has them. A few plain words, no advice. (5) D214's flow cap (rivers, not floods) and D171 (sources only where water begins) stay. (6) **The grey area, answered: preference, not correctness.** Water that hasn't settled within 4 days can't make the game differ from what the editor shows: the file stores exactly the water the editor shows, and the game rebuilds its flows from those depths every tick (FORMAT.md, `ColumnOutflows`) under the same rules the simulation ports exactly, so the water loads as shown and keeps moving as it would have in the editor. It only isn't at rest yet (and a start's pumpable water can come or go while it moves, hence the note). So such places keep their own land and water. (7) **The rebuild** (M9a keeps its priority): the 34 places D214 changed each get whichever version shows the place best, its signature features first (usually its row at 04e90ef; a swap stays where it shows the place better, such as Geirangerfjord at the fjord's centre, or River Coe, Glencoe as the whole glen at 60 m), listed on #35 with the version chosen and one sentence why; second maps that took another sample because their centre failed go back to their centre, titled by #81's rule; places D172 would make tall are noted for the tall round (none built now); the floor's dead trees counted (#82) and shown distinctly on the sheet; tests to the new rule (correctness checks and the floor blocking; the preference checks information, with a test that a short place still builds and says what it lacks); a new review sheet with each place's note, posted on #35, the old one marked out of date; Kyler picks drops from the new one only, for whether a place is interesting enough to play. (8) Kyler, the same day: every first map built from an off-centre sample of its place (about two thirds, e.g. "Near Yosemite Valley (east sample)") gets one line saying why: the centre failed a check (round 1's fallbacks or later), or the sample holds the place's signature better. Where it was a check, the rule above applies (the version that shows the place best, usually the centre), listed with the 34; where the sample shows the signature better, it stays, and says so. docs/PERFECT.md's Real places line 2 becomes "It's a great Timberborn map within a few edits (a water source, a moved start, some trees), not just a pretty copy." | Kyler's review of the round. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-26; amended by D271 (the water follows the real place; drops 43, 47, 73, 82, 93, 100, 106, 108, 110, 111, 119, 123, 129, 137, 150); D300: bed lowered under real water and the overall tilt taken out; a water floor |
| D246 | **Glaciate, a new force** (Kyler, 2026-09-26; recorded in D158; after the forces round 2). Glaciate turns a valley that's already there into a glacial valley: a broad, level floor between steep walls; basins that dip below their own outlet, forming a chain of lakes; hanging side valleys with waterfalls; moraines and an outwash plain built from the material it cut. Kyler: "Carve gives you water; Glaciate gives you land." **Modes:** Flow (click high ground, and it follows the valleys) and Aim (drag, and it grinds through ridges into a pass). **Options:** Power, Size (Auto, as in D226), Meltwater (on by default) and Try another, and nothing more without Kyler's say. It plays out in two acts, about five seconds in all: the ice advances, then melts back and reveals the valley as its lakes fill. It refuses to run over the start, respects D244's ceiling, and keeps and feeds its lakes, so what you see is what you get. No ice-sheet mode for now. Codex prototypes it on `investigation/glaciate` (a PR into `dev`); as with Carve and Quake (D194, D203), when its PR is open and green it is **held, not merged**, until Kyler has tried the demo and says it's ready; then it's adopted onto the shared forces core with the forces round 2's conventions, following its INTEGRATION.md. | Kyler: a force that gives you land. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-26; D265: its demo's camera motion doesn't come across; amended by D291 (round 4 pre-approved; adopted on its own branch for the forces sitting) |
| D247 | **Smooth's "Make walkable" option is removed** (Kyler, 2026-09-26; amends D184, where Ramp became Smooth's "make walkable"). It doesn't do what a player means: a walkable stroke wears steps of 2 or more levels down to 1, then only nominates that ground to the derived-slope planner (`src/core/features/build.ts`), which places slopes by its own rules (joining regions to the start's network, spaced apart), so usually nothing visible happens under the stroke, or a slope appears somewhere else. The shelf's Slope already puts a natural slope exactly where the player wants it (few tools, each doing what you meant: docs/PERFECT.md, the editor). Built on `feature/forces` with the other brush-row changes, for Kyler's next sitting: the toggle leaves Smooth's options row and new strokes never set walkable; operations already saved with `walkable: true` still replay exactly as before (D158), so the engine and the build keep honouring the flag for them. Flatten's ramped edges, which rely on the same planner, are checked in the same round: if slopes don't appear along the rim where a player expects them, the cause and one recommendation go to decisions-pending, and Flatten isn't changed without Kyler. | Kyler: few tools, each doing what you meant. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-26; D270 (#84): Ramped lays its own slopes |
| D248 | **Level lines moves from the brush options row to the view bar** (Kyler, 2026-09-26; amends D182's level lines and D184's options row). Level lines only changes what's shown (a thin line wherever the ground steps down a level), never how a brush works; in the brush row it read as a brush setting beside Square, Precise, Straight lines and Stop at. It belongs in the view bar next to Height colours, the other switch that shows the land's heights: the same label and tooltip, off by default, remembering its state as now, and working whichever tool is picked. Built on `feature/forces` with D247, for Kyler's next sitting. | Kyler: a view switch belongs with the views. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-26 |
| D249 | **Brushes and water sources** (Kyler, 2026-09-26; on `feature/forces` with the other editor changes, for his next sitting). Reshaping land shouldn't leave the old sources in the way: after raising a large area, about twenty sources were left each at the bottom of its own pit and one on a one-tile pillar, flooding the raised land (the brush stroke kept every non-plant object's tiles unchanged, `keptTiles` in `src/editor/Editor.tsx`). (1) **Clear sources**, an option on the terrain brushes (Raise, Lower, Flatten, Smooth, Naturalize): one toggle in the brush options row shared by all five like Square and Precise, off by default and remembered; on, every water or badwater source the brush passes over is removed in the same undo step and its water recedes live; while it's on, the sources under the ring glow red before the stroke reaches them and the ring carries a small mark, so it's never on unnoticed; the forces are unchanged. (2) With it off, **sources ride the ground**: a brush changes a source's tile like any other ground and the source moves with it, as trees, bushes and the forces' objects do; a multi-tile source (a 3×3 BadwaterSource) rides as one rigid, level piece; other objects keep their tiles as now; saved strokes made before this replay exactly as they did (the rule versioned if needed, D158); no brush changes a source's strength or footprint or leaves one buried or floating. (3) **Sources are easy to hit:** with any tool picked, the pointer over water or bare ground within about two tiles of a source targets it, above or under water; a direct hit on any other object wins (a tree on a bank is still the tree); the targeted source's marker shows, with Remove's red glow when Remove is picked; the nearest source wins. (4) **Point and press Delete** (or Backspace): with any tool picked, it removes the targeted source, one undo step, its water receding live, with Remove's sound; a selected source is removed as now. (5) **A Remove drag that starts on a source** (within its targeting range) takes only the sources in its rectangle, whatever the filters say; every other drag works as now; the red glow shows exactly what will go; one undo step; the start always stays. | Kyler: sculpt without the old sources in the way, then add water. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-26; D288: the Remove drag goes with the Remove tool |

| D252 | **M9 is the soul of Dam Good Maps: each stage is judged by Kyler's eye before release** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; extends D115 for M9a, M9b and M9c). Each M9 stage is judged by Kyler's eye against `docs/PERFECT.md` before it is released, not only by the batches, the measures and the probe. (1) **Starts stop looking alike** (M9a, before its review set): the start rules' planting for Minimum starting wood and the berry bushes (D85) is spread over the 20-tile walk, reading the land the way the floor's wood already does (D229), so no two starts get the same ring of groves and berry patches within about 10 tiles; every start still meets Minimum starting wood and the starting-logs floor (D224, D227). (2) **A review set before M9a's release**, from the frozen generator, posted on PR #56 as one comment readable on a phone: the contact sheet (every theme and Any, seeds 1–30 at 128²); the same seeds on the live generator (0.6.x) beside it (`npm run sheet --compare`); 12 maps chosen at random (two per theme including Any, not the best), rendered in 3D from the editor's default view with the start visible; the start-area sheet after (1), added groves marked and dead ones distinct (pending #80), with how many starts have dead groves. M9a is not released until Kyler has reviewed it and said yes; anything he names is fixed in M9a or scheduled into M9b, as he decides. (3) STATUS says honestly where the frozen generator falls short of PERFECT's "Maps" and "Water" sections on that set. (4) **M9b's and M9c's acceptance adds Kyler's eye as blocking**: the same kind of review set, judged against PERFECT's "Maps", "Water" and "Challenge" sections, with every shortcoming named in (3) either fixed or explicitly accepted by him. | Kyler: the generator is what the product is; numbers can pass while the maps still look alike or read as a program's. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; amended by D282 (the generator provides the canvas; M9 judged as a great canvas); D294: M9a approved on its review set |
| D253 | **M10 and M11 are removed** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; amends D158, D159 and D182 where they conflict). The steps that followed them follow the Weather view, in the same order. Removed from the plan: symmetry, and the workshop's symmetric layouts as a target; built-in and user stamps, with their export and import; EDITOR_PLAN's "Stamps and symmetry"; regenerate area; locks (and their conflict rules). The brushes M10 was named for came with Live editing; "the brushes' extras" are Kyler's editor fixes, which go in as he finds them. Kept from M10: Naturalize never breaks `slopes.connect` or a set piece's protected tiles, as tests on the brush as it is now; the naturalness measurement against the official maps stays as information. M11's heightmap conversion moves to Pick a place (D255). | Symmetry: Kyler doesn't want it, and PERFECT asks that every map feel designed by nature, not by a program. Stamps (the catalogue's patterns and user stamps) are presets and copy-paste of land, which PERFECT and D182 rule out; the forces make dramatic land far better. Regenerating an area can't look natural: land shaped by processes acting on the whole map won't line up with a patch regrown against fixed surroundings, making it try is costly, and it goes against the editor's philosophy that the magic happens on the land itself. The forces already reshape part of a map in a way that fits, because they act on the land that's there. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D254 | **The working area** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; a new editor feature; recorded in D158; built with the editor work after the forces round 2, alongside the object brush D235, so it is on the page before Kyler's editor UI audit). (1) The player paints an area with a brush ring; the Select tool's rectangle works too. While it is active, every tool works only inside it: the brushes, the forces and Clear sources (D249). Everything outside is locked, exactly as it is. (2) **Feathered edge:** inside the area a tool's effect tapers off toward the boundary, so edited land meets locked land naturally; never a cliff or a straight wall along the boundary. (3) To the forces, locked land is unbreakable rock (the forces core's hardness): Carve's river turns away from it, lava pools against it, a crater's rim stops at it; nothing a force does is cut off by an invisible line. (4) Water is never locked: it follows the land inside and out (what you see is what you get). (5) While it is active the locked land is visibly dimmed, and one key clears the area, so it is never on unnoticed. (6) Every edit is one undo step as always; marking or clearing the area is not an edit. | It replaces locks and regenerate area (D253) with something that works on the land itself: the player protects what they like and keeps shaping the rest with the same tools. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; amended by D259 (the working area is the Select tool's selection, built on `feature/forces` now) |
| D255 | **Pick a place takes over the conversion** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; amends D159, D160, D166 and D175). (1) It no longer waits for M11 (D253). Its conversion (real elevation to a Timberborn map: heights, rivers, sources, the start, the starting-logs floor) is built for Pick a place from Real places' existing conversion, running in the browser. Its place in the order otherwise stays (after the design pass, D176, D236). (2) It follows Real places' rule (D245): the land the player framed is kept; only correctness and the starting-logs floor gate it; anything short of the preference checks ships with a plain note saying what it lacks. (3) Its quiet retries never replace the player's framing, size or scale to pass the preference checks: it builds what they framed, with its notes, and may suggest nearby framings that would play better, which the player can take or ignore. Only a correctness failure may make it look elsewhere, and then it says so plainly. (4) A "use my own heightmap" upload is not planned; it could be added later on the same conversion if Kyler asks. | The player chose that land; as with Real places, its composition is the point and the editor can fix shortcomings. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; D306: Pick a place's square defaults to 256² |
| D256 | **M12 works through whole maps and the forces** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; amends D134 and D139). Claude no longer regrows or locks areas. For character and new features it steers whole-map generation ("describe the map you want" and its candidates) and uses the forces for local change: "make the north mountainous" becomes Quake's Lift or Erupt, "add a big waterfall" becomes Carve or Unleash. The brushes are for precise edits only. Removed from the planned requests: "lock this area", "regenerate the east third", "make it symmetric" and "put a spiral mountain here"; the force steps (B22–B27) are the model for local change. D134's "keep M12 ready" no longer names M10 or M11. | Follows D253: the tools that regrew or locked parts of a map are gone, and the forces reshape part of a map so it fits. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D257 | **The forces are bound only by nature; the editor makes their result compatible with a good start** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D203 and D206 where they refuse at the start). The forces are nature's tools for redefining the world in a spectacular way: bound only by nature, they feel like magic, not machinery. (1) **Bound only by nature:** a force obeys only what it physically is (what a river, a crater, a fault, a volcano or a glacier does) and the map's physical limits (its floor, D244's ceiling, the file format). It never refuses, stops short or reshapes its result for playability: the start, walkability, the start's water and wood, slopes and paths are not its concern. (2) **The editor makes the result compatible with a good start:** the checks dot shows what a force broke (the start's requirements, the starting-logs floor, water in reach), each with its one-click fix where one exists (move the start, plant groves for the floor); any fix missing for what a force can now break is added. (3) **The start:** a map always keeps exactly one start. Where a force carves, buries or moves the start's ground, the start is carried to the nearest valid level ground instead of the force refusing: Quake's and Erupt's refusals where the start sits (D203, D206) become this, and the same applies to Carve and Craterize, and later to Glaciate's adoption (D246). Built on `feature/forces` with the round-2 work, for Kyler's forces sitting. | Kyler: a force that stops short for the start's sake is machinery; playability is the editor's job, and its checks already say what's wrong and how to fix it. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D258 | **Clean, magic gestures for the forces** (Kyler, 2026-09-27, from his review of Carve, where a white predicted-route line traced the land for Unleash and Aim; recorded in D158). (1) No force draws a predicted route, path, outline, footprint or wireframe on the land, before or during its gesture: Carve's route line for Unleash and Aim, Erupt's fit preview (its breadth, the line out to a flank vent, and its "it breaks out on the flank" and "it grows broader" words), and any similar preview in Craterize and in Unleash on sources are removed. (2) Every click mode (Carve's Unleash, Craterize's Strike, Erupt's Vent, Unleash on a source) is one click: the force starts at once and finds its own way. (3) Aim is a drag in a direction: while dragging, only a thin straight arrow from where the drag began to the pointer shows direction and distance; on release the force goes, finding its own way through the land that way, and the arrow disappears as it starts. (4) A stroke the player draws stays visible as it's drawn, because the stroke is the gesture itself, not a prediction: Quake's fault and Erupt's fissure. (5) What stays: the cursor showing where the force will act, and a short word by the pointer only when a force won't act at all (Erupt's "No room to rise here"). Built on `feature/forces` with the round-2 work, for Kyler's forces sitting. | A drawn prediction turns a force of nature into a plotted machine; the magic is in seeing the land answer. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; its cursor amended by D312 (a faint ring sized to the force's reach; waypoints for Carve and Glaciate) |
| D259 | **Select is easy to find and use, and the working area is its selection** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D184, where Select takes no permanent slot, and D254, whose area is no longer a separate concept). (1) **Findable:** Select gets a small button on the bar beside the brushes; M and Ctrl+drag still open it. (2) **Shapes:** Circle (drag from the centre outward, the radius shown beside the pointer) and Brush (paint the selection with the brush ring, its size the brushes' size) join Rectangle, Freehand and Same level; Shift adds and Alt subtracts in every mode, as now. (3) **Set level:** the level list reaches the map's ceiling (22 under D244), not 16; Ctrl+click on the land while a selection is open takes that tile's level as the target, as Flatten's sampling does; it acts at once, as one undo step, exactly as now. Select's own actions (Raise, Lower, Set level, Dig out, Clear objects) stay exact, with hard edges: they're precision tools. (4) **The working area is this selection**, and it feels seamless: while a selection is open, the brushes, the forces and Clear sources work only inside it, with D254's feathered edge and the forces treating the land outside as unbreakable rock; the land outside is dimmed. "Fence off an area, then paint inside it" is one motion: Ctrl+drag with a brush out makes the selection, and on release the same brush keeps painting, now inside it. One row at a time: when a brush or force is picked while a selection is open, the Select row shrinks to a small chip beside it ("Working inside 40 × 40 · Esc to clear") that expands again when clicked; never two full rows stacked. The selection stays open after a Select action (so the player can keep working inside it) until Esc or the × closes it, which frees the tools at once. There is no second way of marking an area. (5) Built on `feature/forces` with the other editor changes, for Kyler's forces sitting (so the working area moves there from D254's later slot). | Kyler asked for a way to select a large area, circle or square, and force it to one height at once, without knowing the Select tool already does this (M or Ctrl+drag, then "to level" and Set level). Hidden behind a key and a modifier, it failed its own designer. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; extended by D261 (Same level becomes Wand, which also selects water); extended by D264 (Select all; Set, Cut down, Fill up; Max water depth) |
| D260 | **Water that no source feeds recedes at once, and a removed source's marker goes with it** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D197's water journey, the preview's warm start and EDITOR_PLAN §6). The rule is the editor's own: water changes only through its causes. Deleting a source removes its water, including the pools it filled; a lake a force deliberately stored (RetainedWater, D216: Carve's oxbow lakes) is its own cause and stays. (1) After every edit that can change what water is fed (a source removed, weakened or moved; a brush stroke, force or Select action that changes where water can flow), the editor finds the wet tiles that no running source can reach any more on the new ground; stored lakes are exempt and keep their water as now. (2) A stored lake keeps its water only while its hollow holds it: an edit that breaches the hollow drains the lake through the breach; an edit that fills it in removes the water. (3) The tiles found in (1) take the canonical start (dry) in the preview's warm start, so their water visibly drains away as part of the edit's own journey: it starts within a frame or two and, at normal speed, is gone within about a second on 128² and two seconds on 256²; at Instant it is gone at once. (4) A removed source's upwelling, marker and strength label disappear the moment it is removed, never after the water or the background check. (5) The canonical settle still ends the journey and must agree: after any edit, the preview's water once it stops matches the canonical settle's, except where the preview only approximates (roofs), within the parity tests' current tolerances. (6) Measured on 256² River Valley and Highlands, before and after: the time until the view shows no water the canonical settle won't have, for removing a source that feeds a river with pools, cutting a river off with Raise, Carving a cut-off and then breaching the oxbow lake with a Lower stroke, and Kyler's case (a wide sheet of water on flat raised land fed by two sources: remove one, the sheet settles to what one source keeps; remove the other, it drains away). Built on `feature/forces` with the other editor changes, for Kyler's forces sitting. | Stale water lingered: the preview re-settles from its previous water (`src/core/sim/preview.ts`), so flowing water drains but water in hollows would only evaporate over days and the preview stops and leaves it; only the background check's canonical settle removed it, after its 0.7 s debounce and its own settle, and every newer edit restarted that, so while Kyler kept editing the stale water could stay indefinitely, and a removed source's "1.5 water/s" label still showed. What you see is what you get (PERFECT). | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D261 | **Select's Same level becomes Wand, which also selects water** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; extends D254 and D259). (1) Wand reads where the player clicks: a click on land selects the ground joined to it at that level, as Same level did; a click on water selects that river's or lake's water tiles connected to the tile clicked. Badwater counts as water. Water tiles are the tiles the view draws as water, never thin films the view doesn't show, so no bank tile is ever pulled in. The selection is a snapshot at the click; Shift+click adds, Alt+click takes away and Alt+drag subtracts, as in every mode, so a player can trim a river down to one stretch. The modes are Rectangle, Circle, Freehand, Brush and Wand. (2) Everything built on a selection works on it unchanged: the brushes and forces act only inside it (the working area, its feathered edge, the dimmed land outside), and Select's own actions (Raise, Lower, Set level, Dig out, Clear objects) apply to exactly its tiles. While the selection is open, the water under the brush turns clear as it does now, so the bed shows. (3) The snapshot never updates itself as the water moves during a stroke; it changes only when the player changes it or closes it. (4) Built on `feature/forces` with the other Select work, for Kyler's forces sitting. | Kyler wants to flatten, raise, lower or smooth a riverbed without altering the land around it. A per-brush "only water tiles" option would follow the live water as the stroke changes it: a raised bed would pop above the water and stop, and lowering near a bank would let the water spread onto the bank, which would then be lowered too. A selection taken once is stable, and the selection is already the working area. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D262 | **M9b runs at xhigh, like M9a** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; amends D210 for M9b). Now that M9 is the soul of Dam Good Maps (D252), M9b (composition and variety) is built on a definition of its own, `m9b-build` (Opus 5.5, xhigh); `m9-build` (Opus 5.5, high) stays for M9c. HANDOFF §7's table matches. | M9b decides how varied and characterful the maps are, the part of the product Kyler judges most closely. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; D278: M9c is removed, `m9-build` stays unused |
| D263 | **Smart Lower's depth comes from strokes, not from holding; new channels are about one tile deep** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D184's smart Lower). (1) **A new channel** (a smart Lower stroke that starts in or beside water and leaves it): its bed starts one level below the surface of the water the stroke starts from, so the water enters about one tile deep; along the stroke the bed never rises, as now, and where the land beside it falls lower it steps down to one level below that land. Holding never deepens it: however long the mouse is held, no tile is cut below that bed; holding only extends the river. It joins the water it starts from without digging a pit in it. Where it must cut deeper to keep flowing downhill (through a rise, or out of a river whose surface is below the land around it), that stretch is cut as deep as it must be, and no deeper. (2) **A deepening pass** (a smart Lower stroke drawn along an existing channel) lowers that channel's bed by exactly one level, however long it's held; a two-deep river is two passes. (3) Plain Lower, away from water, is unchanged: holding still digs deeper. Built on `feature/forces` with the other editor changes, for Kyler's forces sitting. | Kyler draws rivers with smart Lower by holding the mouse, and the brush's pressure kept building wherever he paused or moved slowly, so riverbeds ended up deeper than is reasonable. Timberborn's rivers are mostly one or two tiles deep, and cattail and spadderdock grow only in one-tile-deep water. Smart Lower also started its bed at the bed of the water the stroke started from, so a branch off a deep river was deep all the way. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D264 | **Select all, Set level's three ways, and Max water depth** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; extends D259 and D261). (1) **Select all:** Ctrl+A selects the whole map (in the Select tool, or with any brush out); every Select action then applies to the whole map, and every action below also works on any smaller selection (a Wand-selected lake, a circle). (2) **Set level's three ways**, sharing its level picker: **Set** (cut and fill, as now); **Cut down** (only lowers ground above the level; ground below it is untouched); **Fill up** (only raises ground below the level; ground above it is untouched). "Cut the whole map down to 16" is Ctrl+A, 16, Cut down. (3) **Max water depth** (a number, 1 up to the map's deepest water): wherever the selection's water is deeper than the number, the ground under it is raised so the water sits at that depth; shallower water is untouched. Water stays a result of land and sources (EDITOR_PLAN §5): the action changes only the ground, then the water re-settles. A lake keeps its surface (its spill level), so it becomes that deep; a river's surface may rise a little, so it ends about that deep, and the report after the action says so in a few words if any water ended deeper than the number. (4) These are precision tools, not forces: exact and hard-edged. Objects and sources on changed ground ride it, as the brushes' do; the start stays valid and is moved to the nearest valid ground only if its own ground can no longer hold it. Each action is one undo step, with a clear label ("Cut 4,210 tiles down to level 16", "Water no deeper than 3 on 1,832 tiles"). Built on `feature/forces` with the other Select work, for Kyler's forces sitting. | Map-wide quality-of-life edits Kyler wants: taking a whole map down to the standard height, filling hollows to a level, and making deep water shallow, without painting every tile. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D265 | **The camera only moves when the player moves it** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; an accessibility rule, not a preference; recorded in D158; amends D197's follow and D199's follow camera). (1) Every camera that moves by itself is removed: Follow on the water bar, Follow on Carve's row (and the follow camera it gives Unleash on sources), and the force driver's follow; their settings, code paths and tests go, and saved preferences with follow on are ignored. (2) No feature moves, tilts, zooms or shakes the camera on its own, now or later: every force (including Glaciate's adoption: its demo's camera-motion option doesn't come across), the water, the weather, the Real places gallery's map view and the Select tool. Visual effects on the land (dust, flashes, plumes, ice) are unaffected; only the camera is. Built on `feature/forces`. | A camera that moves on its own while the player is editing makes Kyler feel sick, and it is a known motion-sickness trigger. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D266 | **The forces keep their own pace** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D197's speed control where it paced the forces). Every force plays at its own designed pace, whatever the water's speed: Erupt's swell as at normal speed today (about four seconds), Carve's run, Craterize's strike and Quake's movement as tuned. Esc and undo still revert at once. Built on `feature/forces`. | The water's Speed setting also set the forces' pace, so Slower dragged a force out and Instant took its moment away; Kyler expected Speed to be about the water only. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; amended by D321 (item 29) |
| D267 | **Drought and Badtide become a day-by-day view; the Weather view is folded into them** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D133 and D186). (1) **The buttons:** clicking Drought or Badtide shows the worst day at once (the hazard's last day), with a progress indication while it's worked out, never a frozen page; clicking it again goes straight back to the map's own water. (2) **The day strip:** while a hazard is shown, a day strip on the water bar shows Day 0 (the map as it is) through the last day, with previous and next, a click on any day, a play button that runs through the days in order, and Speed beside it (D268). Stepping from one day to the next animates that day's water moving, at that speed; Instant jumps straight to the day and stays there; nothing ever reverts on its own. Pause and Skip act on the animation between days. (3) **Length:** a number of days for each hazard, 1 to 30, beside the day strip, remembered; the defaults are Normal's longest (drought 9, badtide 8); changing it updates the view at once. (4) **The start's water:** the lakes and rivers the start's pumps would draw from are highlighted while a hazard is shown, and the day strip carries a marker: in a drought, the day the start's water leaves a pump's reach ("Day 6: your start's water is gone"); in a badtide, the day badwater reaches the start's water or its farmland. (5) **Any water:** hovering a lake or river while a hazard is shown says in a few words when it dries ("Dry on day 6", "Lasts the drought") or, in a badtide, when it turns bad. (6) **Editing while a hazard is shown:** every tool works as usual, and the shown day updates live after each edit, following the water journey's rules: the edit shows at once, the day's water follows as fast as the model allows with visible progress, and nothing freezes or waits for the whole drought. (7) **The model stays the game's:** the same weather rules the current run uses (sources stop in a drought, the water drains and evaporates; clean sources give badwater in a badtide and the ground is contaminated). (8) The rest of D133 (the plain-language summary, the map card's lines about the first drought and badtide, the strategy axes) stays planned in the Weather step, which now builds on these buttons instead of a separate view. (9) Built on its own branch (not `feature/forces`), merged separately, and put on the preview for a sitting of its own; the time to show the worst day and to update after an edit on 256² is measured. | The Drought button played one continuous run (the drought, the water coming back, then the map's own water): hard to pause at normal speed, and at Instant the drought showed for a blink. Kyler wants to see a hazard day by day, see the day the water his start depends on is gone, and keep editing while he looks. Most of it exists (the worker's day-by-day run, the soil and contamination display, the game's hazard lengths). | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; point (6) dropped by D269 (an edit ends the hazard view); D307: hovering flooded floor says "Floods when the river refills" |
| D268 | **Speed belongs to the day strip alone** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; recorded in D158; amends D197's speed control and weather run). The everyday water bar is the status, Pause, Skip, Replay, Drought and Badtide; its Speed control is removed. Water after an edit always plays at today's normal pace (a small edit settles nearby in a second or two); Skip goes straight to where it settles, whenever the player wants. Speed (slower, normal, faster, instant) appears only beside the day strip while a hazard is shown (D267). Clicking Drought or Badtide again shows the new worst day. (2) The live update is not built, and anything already built for it is removed. (3) **A proposal for the Weather step, not built now:** a drought line in the normal view: every lake and river shows a faint line on its shore where its water will stand on the last day of a drought (of the length set in the day strip), a lake that would dry out shows a faint dry tint over its bed, and the start's water is marked a little more strongly; it updates in the background after each edit, like the checks, and never blocks. Feedback from the land itself, not a readout (D184). | Kyler finds live updating inside the drought view overkill; he'll simply show the drought again after editing. A hazard's water computed for land that has since changed would be stale. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D270 | **Kyler's answers to STATUS §1's defaults** (Kyler, 2026-09-27). (1) D259 and D260 are recorded as he meant them. (2) M9a's review set has 14 maps, two per theme including Any. | D271 | **Real places: the D245 review sheet's drops, and the water follows the real place** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; PR #35; amends D214, D245 and #85's last line). (2) **The water, before anything else ships.** 68 of 151 places carried a note (44 "The water keeps moving", 28 "No water a pump can reach from the start"), and on the cards deserts and badlands were covered in sheets of water (Death Valley, Painted Desert, Bardenas Reales, Monument Valley, Badlands) and Ngorongoro was one lake. D245's keep-the-land rule is about land; the water is the conversion's choice, and changing sources only was always allowed. A place's water follows the real place: rivers and lakes where the real place has them; if the conversion doesn't already use observed water (ESA WorldCover, D192, or OpenStreetMap's rivers and lakes in the square), it does, so dry places stay dry except for their real rivers. (3) **Held** until the water is fixed, then shown again on the new sheet for Kyler's eye: 2, 12, 41, 58, 60, 71, 90, 101 and 103. | D272 | **Real places are a canvas to reimagine** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; a third line in `docs/PERFECT.md`'s Real places section, in his words; the former third line is now the fourth): "It's a canvas to reimagine: every tool and force works on it like any map, so a player can put a crater in Yosemite Valley and build a mega dam." | A real place is a starting point, not a museum piece: the editor's tools and forces make no exception for it. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27 |
| D273 | **M9b is rewritten around five outcomes, judged by Kyler's eye** (Kyler, 2026-09-27; amends M9b's plan in ROADMAP; with D252). M9b is done when these hold across the review sets, judged by Kyler's eye against `docs/PERFECT.md` (D252); the measures are information. (1) **A readable water story:** a map's water can be followed at a glance, from where it starts, into a main river or lake system, to where it leaves; a few tributaries, never a tangle of small channels (information: the share of the map's water carried by its main system). (2) **Themes keep their promise:** each theme's signature must emerge, checked like an intention; a candidate without it is not chosen. River Valley: a main river through a broad valley. Canyon: a river cut deep between cliffs for a real stretch. Highlands: high, rugged ground with plateaus and valleys among it. Lake Basin: big lakes that dominate the water. Delta: a river splitting into several channels as it reaches low ground. Islands: land broken by water into islands, in many different layouts. Any: no promise. (3) **Every map has a character:** at least one standout intention on every map (no more maps with none), and its one-line description can say something specific about it. (4) **Any handful differs:** across any six maps of a theme, different openings, water stories and standouts, no two alike; the no-clone and no-archetype measures stay as information. (5) **Nothing looks stamped:** no perfect circles (the crater and round lakes get irregular rims), no ruler-straight lines, and no theme stuck in one template; Islands is fixed here (archipelagos across the map, a sea off one edge, island chains, atolls). (6) **Chaos:** Any at Variety 100 and Verticality 100 must meet the same five outcomes, with wild land but readable water and a describable map, and the batch pass-rate rule holds there too; every review set includes four such maps at 256². M9b runs on `m9b-build` (D262) when M9a is released. | M9 is the soul of Dam Good Maps (D252); the outcomes are what Kyler sees, and the numbers only inform them. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-27; D294: M9b starts from M9a's review set's shortfalls; D298: M9b adopts the game's soil rules |

| D316 | **Compute is a resource, effort matches the stakes, and quality is never compromised** (Kyler, 2026-09-28, feedback item 35; a standing rule, at the top of `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/HANDOFF.md`). It applies to every session, agent and investigation, and to prompts from the planning chat and Codex. (1) Invest in what compounds: reusable code (shared modules such as the drainage and source logic, the forces core, the grouped-sources rule), findings future work builds on (the official maps' measurements, the game's rules, what an investigation learned, including why a round failed), kept concise and easy to find, and the project's history (feedback items 33 and 34). (2) Spend little on what doesn't compound: one-off reports, ceremonial captures and tables, re-verifying by hand what's already verified, speculative edge cases, measures no decision uses. (3) Cheap automated checks stay broad; what's cut is agent time, not safety nets. (4) Deep verification where a failure is expensive and hard to see: the water matching the game, files the game loads, saved projects opening, determinism behind share links, release gates; demos and investigations prove the idea and the feel, and Kyler's eye judges the rest. (5) Reports are short; a measure that drives no decision is information, never a target. (6) The cheapest model and effort that does the job; xhigh only for genuinely hard work. (7) Before any report, measurement or manual check: will it be used again, or does it change a decision or what the player gets? If neither, skip it; where a prompt asks for more, follow it and say so in one line. | Spend on what compounds; never on ceremony; never on quality. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-28; active |

| D332 | **Ping Kyler the moment his attention or input is needed** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; a standing rule; amends HANDOFF §7's pings, which pinged only when asked or when he may have walked away). Every ping is the Windows toast (`tools/notify.ps1`), the two bells (🔔🔔) in chat, and one line on the Progress log (#57). (1) **Ping for:** a decision or question only Kyler can answer, including a plan conflict or a pending default that blocks work; anything ready for his eye (a sitting's checklist, a review set, captures to judge, a preview refreshed for him); an approval (a release, a merge that needs his yes, the UI brief or any written brief); a probe batch waiting for his yes; work stopped or stuck because of something on his side (the machine, allowance, tokens or secrets, Timberborn running); anything broken that affects what he sees or plays (a failed deploy or live check, a reverted release). (2) **Don't ping for** progress, green CI, finished steps that need nothing from him, or information-only findings: those go in the Progress log and STATUS as now. (3) Each ping says in one or two lines what's needed, where it is (link or path) and what carries on meanwhile; several non-urgent asks are batched into one ping. (4) Never wait silently: when work is blocked on Kyler, ping, then carry on with anything that doesn't need him. | Kyler's attention is the scarce resource; it should be asked for at once, and only when needed. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |

(5) **Notifications** (#144): for now only a missed theme promise notifies; readable water doesn't while misses are common; revisited once first maps improve. (6) **The nightly settings experiment** (10 of 34 failing): per failing setting, whether it still visibly changes the map or has gone weak through this batch's decisions; a setting that barely moves the map is fixed, never its target lowered; where a decision deliberately narrowed a setting's range, its target is re-based (D148) and said so. | D336 | **Edits never replay onto new land** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; with #86's fix; settles pending #94; amends D158 where it says edits survive regeneration). "Generate, keeping my edits" and "Discard edits" go; the button is just Generate; their texts join the retired terms. (3) Replaying edits onto the same land stays exactly as it is: undo and redo, reopening a saved project, share links (D158). | D337 | **Parity with Timberborn's fluid editing tools** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; the game map editor's water tools, timberborn.wiki.gg Category:Fluid_editing_tools; with D196, D290, D323 item 46; a new batch on `build-light` (Sonnet 5.5, high), on its own branch off `feature/forces`, after batch 3 (#88) merges). The simulation already models all seven fluid objects (`src/core/sim/model.ts`); the shelf places only two. Added, matching the game's rules and footprints from its data, never guesses: (1) Water Seep and Badwater Seep (2×2), strength like a source, stopping while the water over them is deeper than 0.8 m (the model's rule), so they never fill a crater. (2) Aquifer (3×3) and Ancient Aquifer Drill: an aquifer gives no water at map start (it needs a powered drill), as in the game. (3) Badtide Drain (1×2): badwater only during badtides; it runs in the day-by-day Badtide view (#73) when that lands, and its hint says so until then. (4) A start delay for every origin except aquifers, in the source's More: "Starts: at once" (the default) or after N cycles with the game's countdown days, written to TimeActivatedComponent as the game does; the water view shows a delayed source as not yet running. (5) Negative strength: a source may go below zero, a sink that drains water, as the game allows; the water model and the validators handle it. (6) Strength ceilings as the game's: 8 m³/s per emitting tile (`MAX_STRENGTH_PER_TILE`), so a badwater source reaches 72 and a seep 32; the ops schema and the options follow. (7) Defaults as the game's map editor: a new water source at 1 (the game's maximum is 8, which Kyler had remembered), badwater as its data says (3); seeps, drains and drills at the game's defaults from its data. Each new object gets an original model in both looks, in the stone basins' style (never from the game's files), labelled in Markers; save and load round-trip them exactly, and files the game loads keep its component order. Tests for each rule, and a sample of each object joins the next probe batch (seeps stopping at 0.8 m, a delayed source starting, a sink draining, a drain in a badtide). | The editor places every fluid object the game has, by the game's rules. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |

Everything matches the game's rules, footprints and defaults from its data, never guessed. (1) **Objects:** add the Unstable Core (its explosion radius and the cycle it goes off in, in its options, at the game's defaults and ranges; when selected, what it will clear shown as information) and the Reserve Pile, Reserve Warehouse and Reserve Tank (what each holds and how much, only goods the game allows in that kind, up to its capacity, a pile at most 160, at the game's defaults, written with FixedStockpile as the official maps store it). Natural Overhangs 2×1, 3×1 and 4×1 are not now: they are terrain above terrain and come with the Block tool at 3D step 3 (D279–D281, D335), noted in ROADMAP there in place of "natural bridges (Later)". (2) **Ruins** stay exactly as they are; D235's brush paints a ruin field grown as the generator grows one (`src/core/gen/blobs.ts` growth and `punchHoles`' gaps; `src/core/gen/calibrated.ts`' height, variant and orientation shares and field sizes), so a painted field is indistinguishable from a generated one; a click still places one column at the height in the options. (3) **Thorns:** the same brush, painting patches shaped like the official maps' (measured from them if not yet measured); a click places one. (4) **Natural resources:** Succulent joins the shelf as the game's editor places it; D235 is finished for trees and bushes (the brush's strength sets density from a sparse scatter to a dense grove; painting over plants fills gaps up to that density, never stacking; an Age option for trees, Grown (the default) or Mixed; on dry ground the brush tints amber with a quiet "dry ground: these will die", still allowed); a "Mixed woods" item paints the generator's own Pine, Birch and Oak mix in one stroke; faction-only plants (Maple, Chestnut, Mangrove, Dandelion, Coffee, Spadderdock, Cattail) stay off the shelf, as in the game's editor. (5) **For every item:** brushes follow the terrain brushes' sizing (Size slider and F); the ghost shows what a drag will place before release; one undo step per drag or click; D328's levelling and the objects' fit rules apply; each new object gets an original model in both looks (never from the game's files) and a label in Markers; save and load round-trip exactly, in the game's component order. Tests for each rule (a painted ruin field's height mix, gaps and variants match the generator's within its tolerances; density and Age behave; a click places exactly one), and a sample of each new object in the next probe batch (it loads; a reserve holds its goods; a core goes off in its cycle; succulents survive where the game's soil keeps them). | The shelf places what the game's editor places, the way the generator would. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |
| D339 | **Unstable Cores: what you see is what you get** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; with D338). Unstable Cores are the one object that changes the land during play (a crater in a chosen cycle; a dam that goes with it opens a valley or empties a lake), so they change how a map plays mid-game. (1) The generator stays as it is: its "Unstable cores (advanced)" option under Hazards, off by default; no core-based intentions; nothing in generation changes. (2) With the core's shelf item in D337/D338's batch (`build-light`), nobody places a core blind: when a core is selected (placed by hand or by the generator's option), a **Show after it goes off** toggle draws the map as it will be after the explosion (the land and objects it clears, and the water re-settled from there: a lake draining, a river taking a new course); off returns to the map as it is now. It is a view only: nothing changes until the game plays it. The explosion follows the game's own rule exactly (its shape and what it removes, from the game's data; `entities.ts`' "radius + 1" confirmed or corrected). When the day-by-day Drought and Badtide view (#73) lands, it shows the core's moment in its timeline. The next probe batch checks a sample in the game: the core goes off in its cycle, and the land and water afterwards match the preview. | A core rewrites a map mid-game; the player must see what it will do. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |
| D340 | **Crop map to selection** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; a new editor feature after the forces' release, on `build-light` (Sonnet 5.5, high); with D336, D330's brief §6, D323's items 12 and 44, D325's edge lip). When part of a map plays really well, a player can keep just that part as its own smaller map. (1) A Select action: with a rectangle selected (from 4×4, not necessarily square, up to the map's size), **Crop map to selection** makes the map exactly that rectangle; one step, one undo step. (2) The original is never lost: the full map stays in Your maps and undo brings it back at once (brief §6). It's the same land, so everything inside the rectangle, edits included, comes along exactly; it is not a replay onto new land (D336). It is recorded as an operation, so share links rebuild the cropped map. (3) At the new edge: rivers that now reach it flow off it; a river's head left at the new edge gets M9b's natural lip (`water/edgeLip.ts`) so its water flows in; sources are kept as they are; edge walls show as the checks dot's warning with its one-click fix (item 12); objects cut by the edge are removed; if the start falls outside, the checks dot says "No start" and Save to Timberborn asks for one, as after Delete (item 44). (4) The map's name and "how it plays" line are re-read from the cropped map. (5) Tests: a crop keeps every tile, object and source inside the rectangle exactly; undo restores the full map exactly; the cropped map passes the same checks as any map; a share link rebuilds it byte for byte. | There is charm in small maps: keep the part that plays well. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |
| D341 | **Green before merging, no known-flaky tests, and D318 (2)'s verdict** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; amends D318 (2)). (1) #88's merge into `feature/forces` with one red run is accepted this once (a feature branch, the cause identified); **nothing merges into `dev` red, ever**. (2) Carve's Esc failure is treated as a likely real race: Esc arriving before Fast's result is applied (D321's item 29), so Esc does nothing and the carve lands anyway, breaking "Esc or undo reverts it all instantly". After the probe and before the forces sitting's checklist goes out, it is reproduced deterministically (Esc at every moment of a force's run, for every force), fixed in the code if real, with a test that fails before the fix. (3) Glaciate's pace and any other test that passes and fails on the same commit: the cause is found (a real race, or a test depending on timing it shouldn't) and fixed; **no test stays known-flaky**. (4) **D318 (2)'s trial:** `build-light` runs at medium (`build-light-medium`) for self-contained items, and at high for anything that touches layout or tests across batches. | Green means green; a flaky test hides a real bug. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active; (2)'s Esc on a playing force amended by D344 (A4): Esc skips to the end, undo takes it back |
| D342 | **The editor built so M12 is easy later** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; a standing architecture rule; no M12 work now, D277 stands: nothing Claude-specific is built). (1) Every change to a map is an operation in `ops.schema.json`, in plain terms (tiles, levels, strengths, seeds), validated and rejected with a reason when invalid, never silently clamped; no tool, force, Select action, placement or new feature (the Block tool, crop, cores, the parity objects included) changes a map from the UI directly. (2) All editing logic lives in `src/core/` and runs headless in Node: `src/editor/` only turns input into operations and shows results; `src/core/` turns operations into maps; the core never needs a page, a DOM or a GPU. (3) Every question the editor answers (the checks dot's findings, what a force or core would clear, trees within walking reach, the difficulty levers, what's in an area, where things are) is a plain function in the core returning plain data, used by the UI. (4) Every refusal carries a plain one-line reason in words. (5) Contract tests exercise the core's operations and questions directly, not only through the browser. Applied to everything new and enforced in "The page is the editor" rebuild (which splits `Editor.tsx`); existing code that breaks these is fixed when next touched, not in a separate sweep. In `CLAUDE.md`'s standing rules, and in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` when item 34 writes it. | A headless, operation-based editor is what an AI (or any tool) can drive. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |

The deploy runs the same checks as a release on them and stops if any fails. The five levers' grade bands on the map card are tuned by Kyler once the page is wired up. | No generated bulk in git's history (D195); every deployed first map checked like a release. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active |

| D349 | **The milestone order after 2026-09-29** (Kyler, 2026-09-29; orders ROADMAP; keeps D277 and D342; D279–D281 and D335 unchanged). (1) **Finish the editor as players will know it:** the forces release, M9b's release, the High look's release, the parity batch, "The page is the editor". (2) **3D (terrain above terrain):** the foundations, the view, then Erode and the Block tool (D279–D281, D335). (3) **Polish until mature:** Kyler's editor UI audit and the design pass (D236), and every feature feeling finished; collaboration waits for this, since its first users must meet a polished editor. (4) **Collaborative editing, the next milestone:** two players edit one map live, sharing one ordered list of operations; each browser rebuilds the map from it, so both see identical terrain and water (possible because every change is a deterministic operation, D158, D342). Pure serverless, peer to peer (WebRTC data channels): no server of ours or anyone's in the conversation. Joining is two copy-paste codes (the host's Invite gives a code to send; the guest pastes it and gets a reply code to send back; the host pastes the reply and they are connected), compressed as short as possible, one-click copy, each step plainly worded. Only a public address-lookup (STUN) service is used across the internet, none on the same home network, and no relay; if a network blocks the connection, it says so plainly. One player hosts and keeps the order of operations. Open questions for when it starts: undo with two people, and presence (the other player's cursor, tool and intended action). (5) **M12 (Claude)** after collaborative editing; D277's deferral and D342's architecture rule stand. Nothing for collaborative editing is built now. | Finish, deepen and polish the editor before sharing it, then Claude. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-29; active; placements added 2026-09-30 (Kyler): the Weather view (Drought and Badtide day by day, #73) closes step 1, after "The page is the editor", bringing the High look's contamination veins into the Badtide view (which tiles are contaminated) and the Unstable Core's moment into its timeline, with its sitting then; M13 (problem reports, shortcuts and help, a final performance pass) folds into step 3, polish, all done before collaborative editing's first users; step 4 extended by D362 (the collaborative editing brief); the design pass's placement amended by D384 (into step 1); models by D389; the release gate and editor-core items by D385-D387 |

Then (b) for what's left: the stuck basin's outlet is widened with the smallest possible local cut, an automatic repair like the start spring; it looks natural (a small, irregular widening, as if water wore it, never a straight notch), and it happens as part of the map arriving, within the seconds after the land appears, never after the player has started editing near it. Forces not yet adopted take their place in this order when they arrive. The groups show as three clusters in one row (the design pass styles them). The first-run hint points at Carve. | D355 | **Meander folds into Carve** (Kyler, 2026-09-30; amends D352's forces order; with D321's Carve). Meander isn't a force of its own: it only works on an existing river, so on most of the map it does nothing. Its engine is good (oxbow lakes, a floodplain, cut banks and point bars), so it folds into Carve: Carve gains a **Maturity** option in More (Young, Mature, Auto): Young is today's Carve; Mature carves the river, then ages it with Meander's engine (wider bends, oxbows, a floodplain between the bluffs); Carve used along an existing river matures it (Meander's own gesture). Codex's commit (0ce6785f) merges as an investigation marked "adopted into Carve" (#106). | D356 | **A force always has a visible effect** (Kyler, 2026-09-30; a standing rule for every force, in EDITOR_PLAN; with D257, D355, D354). A force always has a visible effect wherever it's used: never "nothing happened", never "not here". It adapts to where it's used (a slope, flat ground, water, a peak), scaled by Power. A force that only works in one narrow situation isn't a force: it becomes an option on another force, or it's dropped (as Meander folded into Carve and the Landslide was dropped). Every adopted force gets a random-click check proving a click anywhere changes the terrain visibly. Today's forces are checked against it now, and any place where one does nothing is reported. | A force that sometimes does nothing breaks trust. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-30; active; Power 0 clarified by D361 (3): the gentlest still-visible effect, even at max Size |
| D357 | **Custom map sizes** (Kyler, 2026-09-30; built at the end of D349's step 1, after the Weather view; nothing built before then except the probe test). Players choose any width and height, including sizes outside Timberborn's standard ones, for unique layouts (for example 128×512, a long river dividing the map into two narrow halves). (1) Limits: from the game's minimum (4) up to 512 on either side, nothing larger. (2) The size setting offers the standard sizes, a few named shapes ("Long river" 128×512, "Strip" 64×512, "Wide valley" 512×256) and custom width and height boxes; share links carry the exact size. (3) The generator uses the shape, never just stretches: a long map gets a long river along it, a chain of islands down a strip, a canyon running its length; every theme's intentions adapt to the proportions; the start's place suits the shape (at one end for a journey, or in the middle with two directions to expand), chosen per theme. (4) Item 47's must-haves scale with the map, as for 48² (fewer mine sites and no district intention on small or very thin maps; more on large ones); the absolutes never relax. (5) Performance: what grows with area may take longer beyond the standard sizes (generation, the water settling, a force's total time); what the player feels stays at the standard: brushes and forces smooth, with no hitches or glitches and input shown within a frame (the smoothness harness applies at every size), the page never freezing, the first land shown as soon as it exists, the map playing correctly in the game. (6) The camera, Reset view, Top-down and the minimap fit any shape, a 512-long strip included. (7) Beyond 256 on either side, the size setting says plainly that Timberborn's own map editor can't open this size and the game may run slower on large maps; it warns, never refuses. (8) Consistent with the crop tool (any rectangle, D340) and, later, Pick a place (any rectangle of the real world, within the same limits). (9) The limits come from the game: the unmodded game loads maps up to 399×399 (`investigation/WORKSHOP.md`); before the feature is built, a probe batch loads 512×512, 128×512, 64×512 and 512×256 maps, checks their water against our model, measures how smoothly the game runs, and reports the practical limits. It waits for Kyler's YES, from the dedicated probe folder. | Unique layouts from the map's shape. | Kyler's decision, 2026-09-30; active |

A few of these slow-settling lake and sea maps join M9b's next probe batch, to confirm the game's water matches after the longer settle. | Settle the water rather than cut the land. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |

(1) **D356's two gaps, both fixed:** (a) a Carve click at the map's edge carves inward, so the river runs into the map rather than straight off it, with a visible channel even at low Power; (b) a Quake click with Lift makes a short natural fault at the click, its direction chosen by the land, as Slide already does, and Try another varies it; both keep the random-click check green. (2) **Batch B's three:** (a) the plain pointer picks and drags every object on the map, trees and bushes included; a hover highlight shows exactly what will be picked, and a bigger object wins over a tree or bush under the pointer; (b) Delete removes everything inside the selection, underwater ruin columns included, even when a ruin field is only partly inside; columns outside the selection stay; (c) imported maps and real places save as `dgm-<name>`, file-safe, numbered rather than overwriting. (3) **M9b's outlet repair (D350 (b)):** the cut is one contiguous shape that follows the water's path out, a smoothly widened mouth or channel as if water wore it, never blobs on either side of the channel, never isolated tiles or fragments (the Lake Basin capture had both); capped in size as agreed (D358); the two captures are redone once it's fixed. | From Kyler's review of the overnight work. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active; Kyler, 2026-10-01, on the redone captures: approved in principle (Any 128² seed 5 looks natural); where the path turns, the cut follows the bend as a smooth curve, never with arms or stubs, and any cut that still has them is refused; the cap is lowered from 2% of the map to about 200 tiles (the real cuts are 62–102), a map needing more being fixed at the source |
| D361 | **The short forces sitting: ten fixes before the release** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; `docs/feedback/2026-10-01-forces-short-sitting.md`, items 1–10; on `feature/forces`; amends D351's tooltip form, D321's Watch name, D344 A3's band, D356's Power 0). What passed is in the file. (1) Power acts on every force and every mode (Quake's Lift included). (2) A drawn gesture's preview is only the player's stroke, a band along the path, never an area: Quake's fault a narrow band about as wide as its crack, not its reach; Erupt's loop traced as an outline, nothing filled; what the force decides (its reach, its extent) is never drawn in advance. (3) Size sets how far a force reaches, Power how strong it is within that: Power 0 gives each force's gentlest still-visible effect even at max Size, Power 100 the full force; Glaciate's Power makes a clear, visible difference. (4) The start's colour: green fits and meets every requirement, amber fits but misses some (listed), red can't be placed; a placed start changes colour only when something about it changed. (5) When the window loses focus, every held key is released and any stroke or gesture ends cleanly. (6) A source's strength is one number everywhere, the settings row showing the pointed-at or selected source's strength live, the changed source highlighted, and for a row both "this source 0.25 · row 1.0 water/s". (7) Select's Ctrl+click only sets the Level box, never changing the selection. (8) The top right aligned with the compass; Sound a speaker icon (crossed out when muted); Watch renamed **Slow forces** everywhere, "Watch" a retired term. (9) **Tooltips are a short phrase telling the purpose at a glance, then the key** ("Flatten: level the ground · F size", "Slow forces: watch forces unfold"), no second sentence, no technical detail; D351 amended and every tooltip rewritten to it. (10) The More/Less button gets an obvious outline or accent. When all ten are in and green, the preview is refreshed with a checklist of only these ten; the forces are released on Kyler's yes. Also, for the Weather view (#73, end of step 1): the day-by-day view can stay on any chosen day (the drought's worst day, say) without cycling back, and steps forward and back a day at a time. | From Kyler's short sitting. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |
| D362 | **The collaborative editing brief** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, from his Q&A in the planning chat; `docs/COLLAB-BRIEF.md`; extends D349's step 4; with D336, D342, D356). Two players edit one map live, peer to peer, one hosting and ordering operations, forces ordered as seeded gestures computed on the agreed map, rejoining sending the current map plus the edits since. (1) A map starts fully shared. (2) Claims made in Select on unclaimed ground, released in whole or part or offered to the other; claims never change how the map looks (a faint outline in the owner's colour, a toggle hides them) and never go into the saved map. (3) An edit reaching into the other's claim is refused, shown before release in their colour ("reaches into <name>'s area"), the claim stopping it like the Floor (D356 not breached). (4) Water flows as the game would across claims; the other player gets a quiet note when their claim's water changes. (5) Undo takes back only one's own last 50 actions; an undo under the other's later edit is refused and named, never replayed onto changed land (D336). (6) Presence: cursor with name and colour, the tool held, the stroke as it's drawn, then the force playing out; a "Go to <name>" button, never automatic. (7) A drop pauses the session read-only with a clear message; one's own copy can be saved; rejoining carries on exactly. (8) Both can Save to Timberborn at any time; the session is kept in Your maps. (9) Changing the whole map is the host's alone, with a quiet note first, both keeping the previous map in Your maps. Nothing is built before the milestone. | Two players on one map, each in control of their own work. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active *Amended (Kyler, 2026-10-01, from Codex's architecture part 2, #119):* §3's warning while drawing is a quick, cautious one (the stroke's band plus the force's reach), and the exact footprint (190–690 ms at 256²) decides on release; §5's undo guards the ground and objects the other player has since touched, and water re-settles as the game would with the other notified (unchanged water would need a full settle per undo, about 9 s at 256²). Cross-browser sameness waits for the determinism investigation's report. |
| D363 | **Mine-site pads are levelled as the land is shaped** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, answering M9b's question; with D328, item 47; applies §4 step 5's flat pads to M9b's mine sites). At 96², 17 of 140 maps failed an absolute because rugged small land had no reachable level 7×7 square. (1) While the land is shaped, before it is ever shown, the generator levels a mine site's pad as it does the start's: about 49 tiles, lowered by at most one level, on dry ground clear of water, within the start's walk. It is generation, not a repair: no land is replaced once shown. (2) The pad looks natural (D328): a small level terrace whose edges ease into the land around it, never a crisp square notch cut into a hillside. (3) The mine-site requirements stay the same at every size from 96² up: two reachable sites, at least 24 tiles from the start. At 48², item 47's requirements still scale down to one mine site. (4) M9b's measures cover 96² from now on, alongside 128² and 256². |
| D364 | **Deposit approved as an investigation** (Kyler, 2026-09-30, Codex's demo #104 rounds 1 and 2, `investigation/deposit`; merged into `dev` via #114 with Codex's commits only; recorded 2026-10-01; with D352, D356). | D366 | **Cross-browser determinism: adopted before the forces release** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, Codex's investigation #122, `investigation/determinism`; merged into `dev` via #123 with Codex's commit only; with D148, D341, D342, D362). | D367 | **Startup: maps open fast** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, approving Codex's investigation #127 rounds 1 and 2, `investigation/startup`; merged into `dev` as an investigation; with D233, D343, D349). Every first-visit map is editable in 1.30–1.44 s median (1.59 s worst), cold on a typical connection, natively, with byte identity on all six maps and twelve saved projects, pixels matching, and Save and export still gated. Adopted in two parts: (1) right after the forces release (`build`, Opus 5.5, high): opening a stored map loads its stored state without rebuilding (legacy files and files with water still pending keep the rebuild fallback); the renderer warms its shaders and GPU state while the map loads; the checks start after the first editable frame, with every gate unchanged. These help every map opened today. (2) With "The page is the editor": the ready-made first-visit map picker and parallel loading, and the caching, merged into one service worker with the multi-core water investigation's isolation worker.
