| D368 | **The re-check of the ten fixes: six more before the release** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; `docs/feedback/2026-10-01-forces-recheck.md`; amends D361 and D351's tooltip form; with D356). Passed: Quake's drawn fault, Power and Size on Craterize and Erupt, the start's colour, focus, Select's Ctrl+click, the More/Less outline. Fixed on `feature/forces`, each behaviour with a test that fails first; the forces release waits for them. (1) **One habit for every tool:** F with the mouse (and { }) sets Size; [ and ] set strength: Power on every force, strength on Smooth and Naturalize, nothing on Raise, Lower and Flatten (they have a target level instead). This swaps today's keys; every tooltip, the shortcuts reference, the first-run hints and EDITOR_PLAN follow. (2) **Quake's hover** shows only a small cursor marker, never a circle of its reach (D361 (2): a force's own decision is never drawn in advance). (3) **Glaciate:** Power is how deep the ice carves (a light scour to a deep U-shaped valley), Size how wide; each makes a clear, visible difference on its own, and the tooltip says so in a short phrase. (4) **Sources:** the label on the map (Markers), the settings row and the source's real strength always show the same number, updating live with every Ctrl+scroll notch (D361 (6) was still wrong). (5) **The top right is one tidy cluster:** the compass in the corner; the level control beside it on the same line, centred with it; Slow forces and the speaker button directly beneath, lined up with the cluster's edges; even spacing and matching heights. (6) **Tooltips:** the shortcut sits at the end as a small key cap, not in brackets in the middle ("Carve a river" then a key cap 7), for every control with a key. *Added (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* (7) **Craterize is click-only:** a crater is one impact, so a drag never draws a line and never makes more than one crater (today a drag makes a stretched double crater). Pressing and dragging makes one crater where the press began, at the current Size (F, as for every force); its hover shows the crater's size circle, as now. Other forces keep their drawn gestures. A test that fails first: a drag with Craterize makes exactly one crater, centred where it began. *Added (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* (8) **Naturalize weathers what the player paints:** it skipped protected ground (set pieces, the start's bench, rivers, forces' results and exact strokes), and since item 37 every Raise, Lower and Flatten stroke is exact, so on a shaped map it did almost nothing where painted, against D356. Its protection narrows to what must not change for the map to stay correct: the start's pad and the ground under sources and objects. Forces' results, brush strokes, rivers (the water re-settling as usual) and set pieces become fair game: painting Naturalize there means "weather these". EDITOR_PLAN's Naturalize entry follows. A test that fails first: Naturalize visibly weathers a cliff made by a force and one made by Raise. *Added (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* (9) **No force pops in at the end:** Carve with Banks animates its channel but its banks appear all at once when the carving finishes. The banks form as the carving passes, each stretch settling just behind the cut, in Fast and Slow forces alike, so the final frame adds nothing new (item 30). Every force is checked for the same pattern (any part of a result applied as a separate step after the animation; Glaciate's settling was one) and fixed the same way. A test that fails first: the last animated frame equals the final land. *Added (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* (10) **Only the player places objects:** one eruption made Slope objects appear around the cone and far from it (near distant ruins, at the far corners), where nothing changed. No force, brush or editor action, and nothing triggered by one, ever places Slopes or any other shelf object; generator-time repairs stay at generation, before the land is shown. If an edit leaves something unreachable (a mine site, the start's walk), the checks dot reports it for the player to fix, never placed silently. Every force and brush is checked. A test that fails first: an eruption, and every other force and brush, adds no objects to the map. *Amended (Kyler, 2026-10-01, on the captures):* (3) Power 100 is right (the approved glacier: a clean U-shaped floor, its river in a channel), but lower Powers made a broad flattened area with a thin, patchy sheet of water. Lower Power makes the glacier shallower and nothing else: at every Power the floor stays clean and the water stays in a channel or tarns, never a sheet. Power 0 is a light scour (polished, rounded rock, a shallow trough, perhaps a small tarn); the middle a shallower U-shaped valley; 100 the approved glacier. The default stays 100 until the lower Powers look right, then moves to the middle like the other forces. *Added (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* (11) **F+scroll sets strength:** holding F and scrolling sets Power on every force and strength on Smooth and Naturalize, nothing on Raise, Lower and Flatten (their Shift+scroll target level unchanged), alongside F with the mouse for Size; [ and ] stay the keyboard alternative. Plain scroll still zooms; Shift+scroll and Ctrl+scroll keep their meanings. The number shows beside the pointer while scrolling, as for Size. The tooltips, the shortcuts reference, the first-run hints and EDITOR_PLAN follow. |
| D369 | **Islands may be mostly sea; islands in a lake can hold objects** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, from Codex's Islands prototype, `investigation/islands`; amends §11's `water.no_flood`; with item 47, D363). (1) The total water cap becomes per theme: Islands may reach about 65–70% water (cap 0.70), since a sea that's most of the map is its promise; every other theme keeps its cap (0.55 for Lake Basin and Any, 0.35 for the rest, as `rulesFor` has it). Item 47's land guarantees still hold on Islands: the start's level land and water without stairs, two reachable mine sites, farmland. (2) The object planner treated dry land inside a lake's outline as unavailable, so islands in a lake couldn't hold mine sites or other objects; this shared code (Islands, Lake Basin, Any) is fixed once on `feature/m9b`, with a test that fails first, every playability check unchanged. *Confirmed (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* only Islands changes; every other theme's cap stays as it is. If a theme prototype shows its cap genuinely holds its promise back (Delta's channels are the likeliest case), it is raised then, with the evidence. |
| D370 | **Codex's M9b theme audits: the outcome** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; `investigation/islands`, `delta`, `river-valley`, `canyon`, `highlands`, `lake-basin`, `settings`, merged into `dev` as investigations via #143; with D348, D350, D333, D369). (1) **Shared, first: D348 was broken on every map.** The Highlands and Canyon audits found the land changing after the first land is shown on all 60 baseline maps (46–342 tiles each): badwater shaping and the start's levelling ran after the land was shown, dropping rivers could remove the main river, and the source-in-flow repair and River Valley's local completion cut tiles. Every shaping step finishes before the first land is shown, apart from D350's outlet wear; a test that fails first: the land shown is byte-identical to the final land, apart from water and that wear. (2) **Also shared, fixed or reported:** a source whose water reaches a fixed badwater hollow ending the map (hollows admitted only where they survive settled flow, or repairable consistently with D348); the mine-pair room check reserving the pair for the placement that follows; planned river joins that settle into separate water systems; the canyon signature scanning only 2–6 tiles from the centre, missing wide rivers' walls; first land and settled water at 256² missing D333's speed targets. (3) **Adopted one at a time after the D348 fix**, every theme re-measured after each: Islands (majority sea on all 60 maps, all three outcomes 100% at every size); Delta (90% / 100% / 85% at 96² / 128² / 256², faster first land); River Valley's own shaping only (one inflow, joining tributaries, broader floors; 90% / 90% / 85%). Their notes for later: Islands' rim reading as a frame, flat interiors, repeating compositions; Delta's flooding small-map islands, repeating two-loop braids, low irrigation at 256²; River Valley's weak tributaries, water reach, narrow valley signatures. (4) **Held, re-audited after the shared fixes:** Canyon (41 to 42 of 60, new water misses, slower 256²), Highlands (already strong; its 96² gain costs nearly three times the first-land time), Lake Basin (the weakest, 47%; its central catchment reached 67% on the old base but 48% with six absolutes failing on the newer one; the theme most worth a second Codex round). (5) **The settings prototype is held and stays last:** it fixes both nightly checks (Verticality's relief about 3–4 to 12–17 levels on every theme; Lakes adds connected side basins), but 7 of 350 maps fail, some steps don't increase (relief 8 of 140, water 26 of 140) and speed misses the targets; Codex gets a round 2 on the new base once Islands, Delta and River Valley are adopted and the shared fixes are in. |
| D371 | **Carve's river is born as it cuts** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; right after the forces release, not blocking it; with item 30, D368 (9), the smoothness investigation `investigation/performance`). While Carve cuts, the water visibly flows into the new channel as it forms: the water front follows just behind the cutting edge, coming from upstream, in Fast and in Slow forces alike (today the land animates and the water fills in only after the carving finishes). The water shown during the carve hands off seamlessly to the real simulation's water as it catches up and ends exactly where the real water settles: no jump, no pop, no water where the settled river won't be (the last frame adds nothing new). Both looks; reduced motion keeps it calm. It uses the smoothness investigation's findings on how water follows land that's still moving, and the same approach applies wherever other forces move water (Glaciate's lakes, Craterize's crater lakes, the Rift's captured rivers). No frame-rate cost at 256² (the smoothness harness); a test that the final frame's water equals the settled water. |
| D372 | **A river that floods a flat as a sheet: the land is drawn again** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, answering M9b's 256² question; extends D358's sea-shelf rejection to river plains; with D348, D350, D370 (4)). Before a land is shown, it is rejected and another drawn when its river would spread as a shallow sheet, about one level deep or less, over a flat larger than about 5% of the map's area. Deep standing water with banks (lakes, seas, ponds) doesn't count, so Lake Basin's and Islands' water is unaffected; a share of the area scales to custom sizes. Not 15%: Canyon 256² seed 14 floods 5,568 tiles (about 8.5%) and never settles. Both Canyon maps (seeds 14 and 22) must be caught, and the rule must reject no map that settles today. For Canyon's re-audit: a canyon floor wide and flat enough to flood like this already misses the theme's promise (a river deep between cliff walls); it's a shaping problem as well as a water one. *Amended (Kyler, 2026-10-01, D373):* stays off: read from the land and the planned water, it caught neither Canyon map; D373 takes both. |
| D373 | **The two Canyon maps that don't settle, and the 96² starts** (Kyler, 2026-10-01, answering M9b; replaces D372's approach, which stays off; extends D350 (d); with D348, D358, D363). (1) **Canyon 256² seed 22** (six rivers joining through outlets too narrow): before the land is shown, every outlet is checked against all the water that joins it, and any too narrow is widened with the same natural shaping; every theme re-measured. (2) **Canyon 256² seed 14** (a big, deep lake that fills too slowly to settle within 6 days): first, such lakes start full, their starting water stored at the level they'd settle to (as oxbow lakes already are), so the map is kept and the game starts exactly as shown. Only where that still doesn't settle within the 6-day limit, the land is redrawn before it's shown when the lake's estimated fill time (volume against inflow) exceeds the settle; its false positives are measured first (how many good maps it would have rejected) before it is switched on. (3) **The 96² starts that fail since D348:** the start's pad is levelled while the land is shaped, before it's shown, as D363 does for mine sites, as part of the small-map work after the three adoptions. *As built (2026-10-01):* (1) works at the source: each channel below a confluence is cut as wide as all the water it carries there, and 256² has no failing absolutes over 840 maps. (2) With (1) in, no map's water fails to settle, so no lake needs starting full and the fill-time rule stays off (its reading is recorded; its highest values are lakes that settle, so any threshold would only reject good maps). The start-full design is in M9b's progress log if a slow lake appears. |
| D374 | **Glaciate in Fast: the ice carves as it advances** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; right after the forces release, not blocking it; amends item 29's 2-second rule for Glaciate; with D368 (9), D371, the smoothness investigation). (1) Today most of the time goes to the opening circle expanding, then the terrain changes in a rush at the end, so it feels both too fast and stuttery. The ice spreads and carves together, the valley deepening as the glacier advances, so the land change takes most of the time; any opening effect is brief or happens alongside it. Every force is checked for the same imbalance (an opening effect that squeezes the land change into the last part). (2) Glaciate's Fast time is 3.5 s, with easing: slow as the ice grips, steady through, settling gently; the other forces keep about 2 s; Slow forces stays proportionally slower. (3) Any stutter left is fixed with the smoothness work's adoption, checked with its harness on every force at 256², Glaciate first. |

| D377 | **A 20-second live tour of the editor's best controls** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; D349's step 3, polish, with M13's help; with D158, D342, D368 (1), (11)). It advertises the controls that make editing feel amazing but can't be discovered by looking: holding F and moving the mouse to size, holding F and scrolling to set Power, Shift+click to do the opposite (Raise becomes Lower), and Ctrl+click to take a level from the land. (1) A ghost cursor performs a short, choreographed sequence on the map in front of the player, about 20 seconds: for example, carve a river and grow it with F and the mouse; erupt a volcano and scroll its Power up with F; raise a hill, then Shift+click to lower it; Ctrl+click a hilltop and flatten a spot to its level; save to Timberborn. (2) The keys show on screen as they're pressed (F held, the scroll, Shift, Ctrl), so the player sees how, not just what. (3) Offered, never forced: the first visit's one quiet hint offers it ("Watch a 20-second tour"), and it's in the ⋯ menu; it plays on a temporary copy of the current map, so the player's map is untouched; Esc ends it at any moment. (4) Built from stored gestures replayed (every edit is a deterministic operation), using the keyboard shortcuts rather than clicking toolbar buttons where possible, so it survives layout changes. (5) The same sequence is recorded once as a GIF and video for the website, the workshop page and posts. (6) No click-Next walkthrough; per-tool "Show me" demos can come later on the same machinery. *Refined (Kyler, 2026-10-01):* the tour shows two forces only, so the rest stay a discovery: Carve (a river, grown with F and the mouse) and Craterize (a crater, its Power scrolled up with F). The rest is the brushes' flow, which players will use most: Raise a hill and size it with F; Shift+click to lower; Ctrl+click a hilltop to take its level and Flatten a spot to it; Smooth the edges; then Save to Timberborn. Still about 20 seconds, live on a copy of the map, keys shown as they're pressed, offered never forced. No second "More forces" tour. *Restated by Kyler later on 2026-10-01, no change; the tour comes with M13's help, in polish, and per-tool "Show me" demos can follow on the same machinery.* | The best controls are invisible until shown. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |

| D379 | **The code's licence is the GNU Affero General Public License v3, or any later version** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; replaces MIT). Dam Good Maps is a website, and the AGPL asks anyone who runs a modified version as a website or service to offer its full source to its users, as well as anyone who distributes one. (1) `LICENSE` is the official AGPL v3 text from gnu.org, unedited; `package.json`'s `license` is `AGPL-3.0-or-later`. (2) The README's licence section says it in a few plain lines: free to use, study, modify and share; modified versions, distributed or run as a service, must offer their full source under the AGPL; the maps people make belong to them and aren't covered by the code's licence; versions published before 2026-10-01 stay under MIT; copyright (c) 2026 Timbermods. (3) Both pages' footers link the source ("Source", to the repository), as the AGPL expects of a hosted service. (4) Third-party dependencies keep their own licences. A PolyForm Noncommercial licence was proposed and cancelled the same day, before anything was committed; nothing of it remains. | Free software that stays free, including as a website. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |
| D380 | **Performance is a critical requirement for perfection** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; with D316–D318, PLAN §10's budgets and D345). Slowness in generation, the forces, batch jobs, analysis or the editor degrades the experience, so it is part of what perfect means, not polish. (1) Every feature meets its speed budget before it ships. (2) A speed regression blocks a merge like a failing test: the budget is the gate, measured in a quiet window (the smoothness investigation's harness is the gate for the renderer and moving water). (3) It is written into `docs/PERFECT.md` and `CLAUDE.md`'s standing rules. | A fast tool is part of a perfect one. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |
| D381 | **The Rust order** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; Codex's `investigation/rust-water`, #156, byte-identical everywhere; the performance audit, #152). (1) **The Rust water port** proves the toolchain, the bridge and the identity tests; it is approved: native builds serve batch jobs now (M9b's measures, theme measures, nightly checks; the 840-map settle batch drops from 281 s to 146 s), without waiting for M9b's release; in the browser Chromium uses the Rust settle (about 1.7–2.5× faster) while Firefox and WebKit keep TypeScript until a short Codex round explains Firefox's slowdown (its WebAssembly was about 4× slower, WebKit's slightly slower); threading stays experimental; Rust 1.90, the wasm32 target and the Rust build join CI and the setup command. (2) **The five released forces are ported to Rust**, byte-identical; their TypeScript is tagged `ts-forces-final` and then deleted. (3) **The forces' planning, and the analysis and checks, move to Rust.** (4) **The Rift, Deposit and Carve's Maturity are adopted directly in Rust**, and every later force (Erode, future Codex demos) is built in Rust. (5) **The generator**, after M9b's release. (6) **The editor's operations and undo**, only if the performance audit shows the boundary cost justifies it. The interface and the rendering stay in TypeScript. Each later port gets its own tag before its TypeScript is deleted; every port passes the identity tests (D366) and its speed budget (D380). | One fast core, ported in the order that pays. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |
| D382 | **No outside users for now** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; with D379). Dam Good Maps is used only by Kyler. The one exception: Kyler's own saved maps and projects keep opening, or are converted automatically. To be revisited before the tool is promoted to other players. | Free to make it right, with no old promises to keep. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |
| D383 | **The dam sketch tool, and later the reservoir finder** (Kyler, 2026-10-01; D349's step 1, after the Weather view; needs the Rust water, D381, and "The page is the editor"; replaces D287's guessed dam sites). (1) The player draws a wall of any shape and height, using the game's dam, levee and floodgate rules, and the reservoir fills behind it, simulated with the game-exact water; it reports the water held, the days of drought covered, the tiles needed and what it floods. (2) Nothing is suggested or guessed, and nothing is saved unless real objects are placed. (3) Later, on the same engine: **the reservoir finder** shows each basin's storable water and the exact tiles to wall, every candidate checked by simulation. | Dam planning that is simulated, never guessed. | Kyler's decision, 2026-10-01; active |
| D384 | **The design pass moves into step 1** (Kyler, 2026-10-02; amends D349's placement, D236 and D113's visual-design line).
